7-20 Page Los Alamos National Laboratory

8 Conclusions
A new suite of critical experiments has been designed to validate Zr and ZrHx nuclear data for
nuclear criticality safety applications. The proposed suite of experiments contains a total of ten
HEU-fueled configurations highly sensitive to Zr/ZrHx cross section and angular distribution data
across the thermal, epithermal, intermediate, and fast neutron energy regions (Table 3-1). Of the
ten total experiments, six target Zr cross sections directly, while the remaining four target ZrHx
thermal scattering data. The K-PSO algorithm was used to determine the optimal neutron moder-
ator and radial neutron reflector materials, and corresponding dimensions, for Zr radiative neutron
capture cross section sensitivity in each aforementioned neutron energy region. The resultant ma-
terial selection is presented in Table 8-1; all of these configurations also include a Zr metal axial
neutron reflector. Note that the sixth experiment is an unreflected fast experiment which maxi-
mizes Zr elastic scattering angular distribution sensitivity and partially decouples sensitivities from
corresponding integral cross sections (Section 7.1.5).

 Sensitivity Sensitivity
  Energy Region Radial Neutron Reflector Neutron Moderator
 Zr(n,γ) Zr(n,elastic)
 Thermal Polyethylene Polyethylene -0.02005 -0.03053
 Epithermal Zirconium Polyethylene -0.00359 0.02829
  Low-Intermediate Zirconium Beryllium -0.02430 0.04277
 Intermediate Zirconium Beryllium -0.04535 0.13477
        Fast                     Zirconium                           N/A                 -0.03787           0.40007
        Fast*                       N/A                              N/A                 -0.01473           0.32050
 * ESAD absolute sensitivity achieved is 0.3649 compared to 0.0928 in the fast reflected design.

 Table 8-1: Final preliminary experiment designs for the Zr metal configurations of IER 516: ZTA
 .

 Above the thermal energy region, the K-PSO algorithm identified that a large Zr metal radial
neutron reflector (analogous to the Zeus copper reflector) was required to achieve maximal sensitivity
to Zr nuclear data. Moreover, all but five of the top 25 configurations in each respective energy
region employed the large Zr reflector (Sections 6.1.2/6.1.3). Similarly, Be neutron moderator
materials were preferred in both the majority of the top 25 intermediate configurations and the low-
intermediate configuration, with Teflon® being the secondary option. The reduced lethargy gain
per collision and additional (n,2n) contribution from Be produced the optimal neutron spectrum
for sensitivity to Zr cross section data in the intermediate energy, or Zr resolved resonance region
(Section 6.1.3). Furthermore, the dependence of neutron moderation on a single element, which
possesses well-constrained nuclear data, is optimal and reduces the potential for inadvertent biases
and/or compensating errors. Implementation of the aforementioned materials in each proposed
experiment will enable sensitivities to Zr cross section and angular distribution data which far
exceed current benchmark experiments (Section 7).
 Four new critical experiments, incorporating two distinct experimental configurations were de-
signed to validate ZrHx thermal scattering law evaluations. The first of which includes the high
HEU mass Jemima plates while the second utilizes the low HEU mass Class Foils (Table 8-2). Two
experiments, one with delta(δ)-phase ZrH1.6 and the other with epsilon(ϵ)-phase ZrH2 , are recom-
mended on each experiment platform to validate the new (δ)-phase and epsilon(ϵ)-phase ZrHx TSL
evaluations included in the ENDF-8.1 library. This new suite of ZrHx experiments will provide

Los Alamos National Laboratory Page 8-1

Conclusions

well-characterized benchmark experiments, each of which possessing both ZrHx TSL sensitivities
exceeding all those currently available (Section 7.2), and extensive ZrHx phase characterization in-
formation which has historically been omitted. Finally, the additional anticipated cost of procuring
and hydriding the Zr metal required for these experiments, along with strong interest from collab-
orators in additional experiments, suggests this suite of experiments could become a separate IER
to ensure the outcomes of the experiment campaign meet the needs of all entities.

     15/6 Jemima (65.4)     Delta(δ)-ZrH1.6       0.07188            0.07467             0.00888            0.01248
     15/6 Jemima (65.4)     Epsilon(ϵ)-ZrH2       0.09307            0.09444             0.00925            0.01325

Table 8-2: Newly designed ZrHx critical benchmark experiment details. Absolute ZrHx TSL sensitivities reported span the 1e-5
 eV to 2 eV neutron energy region.

 The objective of this preliminary experimental design was to identify the optimal neutronic
benchmark experiments for Zr and ZrHx nuclear data validation. With this criteria met, it is
recommended to move on to the final experiment design phase (CED-2) of this IER. During which,
additional considerations such as procurement costs, engineering constraints, facility inventories,
and operational safety will be utilized to refine the optimal neutronic experiments identified in this
analysis into the final experiments to be performed at NCERC.

Los Alamos National Laboratory Page R-5

A Additional Nuclear Fuel Selection Information
Several nuclear fuels were considered for use in the ZTA experiment designs. Although HEU fuel
was ultimately implemented in the preliminary design, Pu fueled systems were demonstrated to
increase sensitivities to Zr nuclear data above 1 MeV.

 (a) (b)

 Figure A-1: (a) Sensitivity to Zr elastic neutron scattering cross sections, and (b) sensitivity to Zr inelastic neutron scattering
 cross sections in the fast ZTA reflected experiment and an analogous critical experiment designed using the Plutonium
 Aluminum No-Nickel (PANN) plates.

 Several HEU fuel options were considered in the design process, such as different arrangements
of the Jemima plates/rings, the smaller outer diameter (6 in.) C disks, and the hemispherical
Rocky Flat shells [34]. The Jemima plates were selected due to both the reduced favorability of
the cylindrical geometry (which increases sensitivity to test materials as shown in Figure A-2), and
the ease of manufacturing discs compared to spherical components. Different arrangements of the
HEU Jemima plates, such as only using the Jemima rings, were not observed to increase sensitivity
to the Zr test material in the reflected fast configuration compared to a nominal stack of full 21 in.
plates.

D Additional ZrHx Experiment Comparisons
Below are comparisons of ZrHx thermal scattering law sensitivities of the delta(δ)-phase ZrH1.6
experiments designed using the HEU Class Foils and Jemima Plates. The trends are similar to
those observed in the epsilon(ϵ)-phase ZrH2 experiments. Figure D-2 on the following page provides
comparisons of ZrHx thermal scattering law sensitivities of the delta(δ)-phase ZrH1.6 experiments
designed to benchmarks available in the ICSBEP. The trends are also similar to those observed in
the epsilon(ϵ)-phase ZrH2 experiments.

E Additional Low Intermediate Sensitivities
Figure E-1 contains elemental Zr elastic neutron scattering and radiative neutron capture sensi-
tivity profiles for each optimized reflector-moderator material combination considered for the low-
intermediate experiment design. The elemental Zr inelastic cross section and ESAD (as both a
function of incident neutron energy and outgoing neutron angle) sensitivity profiles of these same
designs are presented in Figure E-2. All configurations include both Zr metal radial and axial neu-
tron reflectors, and the Be moderated configuration was selected in the final preliminary experiment
design since the greatest Zr (n,γ) sensitivities are enabled between 100 eV and 5 keV.
